They do this through holy instruction in the wisdom <note>original: "science"</note> of holy men who have already been initiated into the higher mysteries. In this way, each rank of the Hierarchical Order is led, according to its own degree, toward Divine cooperation. They achieve this by performing, through grace and God-given power, those mysteries which exist essentially and beyond-essentially <note>original: "super-essentially"</note> within the Godhead. These mysteries are accomplished by God in a way that is beyond nature, yet they are revealed to us through our Hierarchy so that we might imitate the "God-loving minds" <note>The Holy Angels</note> to the highest extent possible.

# CHAPTER IV. <note>original: "CAPUT IV"</note>

## *What is meant by the name <note>original: "appellation"</note> "Angel"?*

Now that the nature of the Hierarchy has been, in my judgment, sufficiently defined, we must next praise the angelic Hierarchy. We must contemplate with heavenly vision the sacred descriptions of it contained in the scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note>, so that we may be raised up through these symbolic <note>original: "mystic"</note> representations to their most God-like simplicity. There, we may praise the Author of all Hierarchical wisdom with a reverence worthy of God and with thanks to the source of all mysteries. 

First of all, however, let this truth be spoken: it was through goodness that the Godhead (who is beyond essence), having established the nature <note>original: "essence"</note> of created things, brought them into being. For it is the unique characteristic of the Cause of all things—and of a Goodness that surpasses all—to call created things to share in itself, according to the capacity <note>original: "analogy"</note> determined for each order of existence. 

All created beings that exist share in a Providence that overflows from the Godhead, who is beyond substance and the Creator of all things. Indeed, they would not exist at all unless they participated in the essence and origin of created things. All inanimate things, then, participate in God simply by their existence; for the "Being" of all things is the Godhead, which exists above all Being. Living things participate in the life-giving power which is itself above all life. Rational and intellectual beings participate in the self-perfect and pre-eminently perfect wisdom which is above all reason and mind. It is evident, then, that all those Beings who share in Him in various forms dwell around Him. Therefore, the holy ranks of the heavenly Beings share in the Divine gifts in a higher...

<summary>This page concludes the definition of Hierarchical roles and begins Chapter 4, explaining that all created things exist by participating in the Godhead's "Providence" according to their specific capacities (existence, life, or intellect).</summary>
<keywords>Celestial Hierarchy, Godhead, Providence, participation, Angels, Chapter IV, creation, Divine goodness</keywords>

--- Page 31 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter IV, explaining why the heavenly beings are specifically called "Angels." It transitions from discussing God's goodness in creating the Hierarchy to how these celestial beings are uniquely suited to receive and transmit Divine light compared to other forms of existence.</meta>

<page-num>24</page-num>

->CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.<-

...rank than things which merely exist, or which lead an irrational life, or which are rational like ourselves. For by shaping themselves intellectually to imitate the Divine, and looking away from earthly things toward the Divine likeness—striving to mold their own spiritual likeness after its example—they naturally have a more generous communication with God. They are near to Him and ever moving upwards as far as is permitted. They lift themselves up with the intensity of steady Divine love and receive the first illuminations without any worldly blemish. By conforming themselves to these lights, their whole life becomes spiritual.

These, then, are the ones who, firsthand and in many forms, participate in the Divine. They are the first, in various ways, to make known the hidden mysteries of the Godhead. Therefore, above all others, they are considered pre-eminently worthy of the title "Angel" <note>original: "angelic appellation"; the word "Angel" literally means "messenger"</note> because the Divine illumination comes to them firsthand, and through them, revelations that exceed our natural abilities are passed down to us.

In this way, the Law—as the Word of God <note>the Scriptures</note> affirms—was given to us through the ministry of Angels.<sup>1</sup> Angels also led our famous fathers<sup>2</sup> <note>the Patriarchs, such as Abraham or Jacob</note> both before and after the Law was given to God. They did this either by leading<sup>3</sup> them in what was to be done and turning them away from error and an unholy life to the straight way of truth,<sup>4</sup> or by making known to them sacred ordinances,<sup>5</sup> hidden visions of heavenly mysteries,<sup>6</sup> or certain Divine predictions through the Prophets.<sup>7</sup> 

But if anyone should say that Divine revelations were made directly and immediately to certain holy men,<sup>8</sup> let him learn clearly from the most holy scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note> that no one has seen, nor ever shall see, the hidden nature of God as it truly is.<sup>9</sup> Instead, Divine revelations were made to those holy men in a way that suits God’s way of revealing Himself—that is to say, through certain holy visions adapted to the capacity of those who saw them. 

Now the Word of God, in its full wisdom, names that special vision a "Divine manifestation" for the instruction of those who contemplate the Divine. This vision reveals the Divine image as if it were clothing things that have no form in a recognizable shape. It is as though a Divine illumination were given to the spectators through this vision, and the godly persons themselves receive a kind of religious initiation. Our famous fathers were instructed in these Divine visions through the mediation of the heavenly powers. Does not the teaching given to us in the scriptures describe the holy legislation of the Law, given to Moses, as coming straight from God? It does this to teach us the truth that the Law is a symbol of a Divine and holy plan <note>original: "economy"; here meaning the Divine administration or arrangement of the world</note>. But the Word of God, in its...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how Divine wisdom is communicated through a structured hierarchy of Angels, transitioning from the general principle to specific biblical examples involving Gabriel, Joseph, and Jesus Himself.</meta>

<page-num>25</page-num>

-># CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.<-

Wisdom also teaches us this—that it came to us through the mediation of Angels. It is as if the Divine regulation established this rule: that through those in the first rank, those in the second rank are brought to God. For the supreme Law-giver has established this law not only for superior and inferior minds, but even among those of the same rank: within each Hierarchy, there are first, middle, and last ranks and powers. Those who are more Divine serve as the instructors and guides for those below them, leading them toward Divine access, illumination, and participation.

I also observe that Angels were the first to reveal the Divine mystery of Jesus’s love for humanity, since the gift of knowledge was passed to us through them. For example, the most Divine Gabriel instructed the High Priest Zachariah¹ that, by Divine grace and beyond all hope, a son would be born to him. This son was to be a prophet of the human-like work of God performed by our Jesus² <note>original Greek: "ἀνδρικῆς τοῦ Ἰησοῦ θεουργίας" (andrikēs tou Iēsou theourgias); this refers to the "theurgy" or divine-working of Jesus in his human form</note>, which was to be revealed to the world for its salvation, as befits Divine goodness. He revealed to Mary,³ however, how the Divinely-originated mystery of the unutterable "God-formation" <note>the process of God taking human form</note> would take place within her.

Yet another Angel instructed Joseph⁴ on how the promises Divinely made to his ancestor David were truly being fulfilled. Another Angel announced the news to the shepherds⁵—who were purified by their separation from the crowds and their quiet life—and with him a multitude of the heavenly host proclaimed to those on earth that famous hymn of praise <note>original: "doxology"</note>.

Let us then ascend to the highest manifestations of light found in the holy scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note>. For I perceive that even Jesus⁶ Himself—the beyond-substantial <note>original: "supersubstantial"; existing beyond the nature of any created substance</note> cause of the highest heavenly Beings—did not bypass the good order established for mankind when He took on our human condition. Although He remained unchanged in His own nature, He readily subjected Himself to the arrangements of God the Father through the mediation of Angels. Through their mediation, the Son’s departure to Egypt⁷ (arranged by the Father) was announced to Joseph, as was the later return to Judea⁸ from Egypt.

We see Him further subjecting Himself to the Father's decrees. But I will say no more on this, as I am addressing someone who already knows our hierarchical tradition concerning the Angel⁹ who strengthened Jesus Himself. You also know that when Jesus came to reveal the good work of our salvation, He Himself was called the "Angel of Great Counsel"¹⁰ <note>This title for the Messiah comes from the Septuagint (Greek) version of Isaiah 9:6</note>. For, as He Himself says in the manner of an Angel <note>meaning "as a messenger"</note>, "Whatever He heard from the Father, He declared to us."¹¹

<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

### CHAPTER 5
<note>original: CAPUT V</note>

->*Why all the heavenly beings are called by the common name of "Angels."*<-

This, then, in our opinion, is the reason for the angelic title given in the Scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note>. We must now inquire why the theologians call all <gloss>1</gloss> the heavenly beings together "Angels," but when they provide a more accurate description of the celestial ranks, they use the name "Angelic Order" specifically for that group which completes the full number of the Divine and heavenly armies. Before this rank, they list the superior orders of Archangels, Principalities, Authorities, and Powers, as well as all the other orders that the revealed tradition of the Scriptures recognizes as superior to the Angels. <gloss>2</gloss> 

We affirm that throughout every sacred order, the superior ranks possess the light and powers of the subordinate ranks, but the lowest orders do not have the same powers as those who are above them. The theologians, then, call the most holy ranks of the highest beings "Angels" because they also reveal Divine light. However, there is no reason to call the lowest order of the celestial minds "Principalities," "Thrones," or "Seraphim" <note>original: "Seraphin"</note>. This lowest order does not possess the highest powers; instead, through its own specific role, it leads our inspired <term>Hierarchs</term> <note>religious leaders or high-ranking officials in the sacred hierarchy</note> toward the splendor of the Godhead as it is known to them. Thus, the holy powers of the beings above this rank act as guides toward the Divine for that order which completes the angelic hierarchies. 

Perhaps some might also say that all angelic names are shared, since all celestial powers possess, to a greater or lesser degree, a similarity to God and the gift of light from God. However, so that the question may be thoroughly investigated, let us reverently examine the holy characteristics assigned to each celestial order in the Scriptures.

---

### CHAPTER 6
<note>original: CAPUT VI</note>

->*Which is the first order of the celestial beings? Which is the middle? And which is the last?*<-

How many and what kind of orders of heavenly beings there are, and how their hierarchies are organized among themselves, I believe only the Divine Author of their creation truly knows. Furthermore, they themselves know their own specific powers and light...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the celestial hierarchy, transitioning from the general use of the name "Angel" to a specific breakdown of the nine ranks into three distinct triads based on their proximity to God.</meta>

<page-num>27</page-num>
<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

...and their sacred and otherworldly <note>original: "supermundane"</note> regularity. For it is impossible for us to know the mysteries of these heavenly minds and their most holy perfections unless the Divine <note>original: "Godhead"</note> reveals them to us through those who perfectly understand their own condition. We, then, will say nothing of our own accord; instead, we will set forth the angelic visions seen by the holy Prophets of God, interpreting them as best we can. 

The Word of God has designated all nine celestial beings by names that show their functions. Our Divine Teacher <note>original: "Initiator"; likely referring to the author's mentor, Hierotheus</note> divides these into three groups of three. He says that the first group is the order which tradition declares to be always around God, united closely and immediately to Him before all the rest. He says that the teaching of the holy scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note> declares that the "Most Holy Thrones" and the "many-eyed" <note>Ezekiel 1:18</note> and "many-winged" <note>Ezekiel 1:6</note> ranks (named in the Hebrew tongue as "Cherubim" <note>Ezekiel 10</note> and "Seraphim" <note>Isaiah 6:2</note>) are established immediately around God, with a nearness superior to all others. 

This first group, then, our famous guide affirmed to be a single triad of equal rank. It is truly the first order, for there is no other more God-like or closer to the initial light of the Godhead. He says that the second group is composed of the **Authorities**, **Lordships** <note>often translated as Dominions</note>, and **Powers**. The third group, being the lowest of the celestial hierarchies, is the order of the **Principalities**, **Archangels**, and **Angels**.

---

### CHAPTER 7
<note>original: CAPUT VII</note>

->*Concerning the Seraphim, Cherubim, and Thrones, and*<-
->*concerning their hierarchy, which is first.*<-

While we accept this arrangement of the hierarchies, we affirm that every name given to the celestial minds denotes the God-like characteristic of each rank. Those who know Hebrew affirm that the holy name of the **Seraphim** denotes that they are "fiery" or "burning," while that of the **Cherubim** denotes a "fullness of knowledge" or a "stream of wisdom." 

Naturally, then, the first of the heavenly hierarchies is served in a holy manner <note>original: ἱερουργεῖται (hierourgeitai)</note> by the most exalted beings. This rank is higher than all others because it is established immediately around God, and the first Divine manifestations and perfections reach it earlier than the others, as it is the nearest. They are called "Burning Ones," "Thrones," and "Streams of Wisdom"—names that explain their God-like nature. The name "Seraphim" plainly teaches their ever-moving...

--- Page 35 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of the first triad of the celestial hierarchy—the Seraphim, Cherubim, and Thrones—moving from the symbolic meaning of their names to the nature of their specific rank and purity.</meta>

<page-num>28</page-num>
<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

...around things Divine, with constancy, warmth, and keenness. They possess the seething energy of a persistent, indomitable, and unceasing perpetual motion. They vigorously transform those below them through precept and example, giving them new life and rekindling them to that same heat. They purify through fire and burnt offerings <note>Dionysius uses the imagery of fire and sacrifice to describe the spiritual refinement the Seraphim provide.</note>, possessing a light-like and light-shedding nature which can never be concealed or extinguished, and which remains always the same as it destroys and dispels every kind of obscure darkness. 

But the name of the **Cherubim** <note>original: "Cherubin"</note> denotes their knowledge and their vision of God, and their readiness to receive the highest gift of light. It signifies their power to contemplate the Divine beauty <note>original: "comeliness"</note> in its first revealed power, and their being filled with the wisdom that is shared with them, which they ungrudgingly communicate to those ranks next to them by pouring forth the wisdom given to themselves. 

The name of the most exalted and pre-eminent **Thrones** denotes their manifest exaltation above every grovelling inferiority and their celestial tendency toward higher things. It signifies their unswerving separation from everything remote from God, and their invariable and firmly fixed settlement around the truly Highest with the full force of their powers. It also denotes their capacity for receiving the approaches of the Godhead, because they are free of all passion and earthly tendencies; they bear God <note>The name "Thrones" implies they serve as the seat or support of the Divine presence.</note> and expand themselves with burning desire for Divine receptions. 

This, then, is the explanation of their names, so far as we can give it; but we ought to say what we think their Hierarchy is. For I suppose we have sufficiently shown above that the purpose of every Hierarchy is an unswerving devotion to the imitation of the Divine Likeness, and that every Hierarchical function is set apart for the sacred reception and distribution of an undefiled purification, Divine Light, and perfecting knowledge <note>original: "science"; here meaning systematic spiritual knowledge or *scientia*</note>.

But now I pray that I may speak worthily of the most exalted minds—how their Hierarchy is revealed through the Holy Scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note>.

One must consider, then, that the Hierarchy belongs properly, and is in every respect like, to the first Beings, who are established immediately after the Godhead who gave them Being. They are marshalled, as it were, in His very vestibule, surpassing every unseen and seen created power. Now, so far as they are pure, we must regard them not as though they have been merely freed from unholy stains and blemishes, nor as though they were simply unreceptive of earthly imaginings. Rather, they are exalted far above all stain of inferiority and every temporary holiness, in accordance with the highest degree of purity. They are established above even the most God-like powers, clinging unflinchingly to their own self-moved and consistent order in their invariable love of God. They are conscious in no respect whatever of any decline <note>original: "declivity"</note> to a worse condition, but possess the invariable fixity of their own God-like identity—never liable to fall, and always unmoved.

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the first triad of the celestial hierarchy, specifically detailing how the Seraphim, Cherubim, and Thrones engage in contemplation and receive direct illumination from the Godhead.</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>
# CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.

Again, since they are contemplative beings, we must not think of them as perceiving physical signs <term>original: "sensible signs"</term> with their minds. Nor should we think they are led to the Divine by the various holy representations written for our own meditation. Instead, they are filled with unearthly knowledge of a higher light and are satisfied, as much as is permitted, with that supreme beauty which is beyond all being <term>original: "superessential"</term> and revealed in the Trinity. 

Because they have been deemed worthy of communion with Jesus, they do not express this divine likeness through physical forms or sacred images. Instead, they are truly near to Him, enjoying the first-hand knowledge of His divine illuminations. Furthermore, because they imitate God to the highest possible degree, they participate—as much as possible—in His divine and loving virtues <note>original: "philanthropic virtues," referring to God's love for humanity.</note> through a direct manifestation of His power. 

Regarding their perfection, we must not imagine they are enlightened by systematic logic <note>original: "analytic science."</note> or subtle study. Rather, they are filled with a primary and supreme deification <note>In this context, deification (theosis) refers to the process of being made more like God through His grace.</note> that comes from the most exalted knowledge of divine actions that any Angel can possess. They are not guided in spiritual matters by other holy beings, but by the Godhead itself. They are raised directly to God by their own power, order, and rank, which surpasses all others. 

These beings are established near the All-Holy without any variation or change. They are led to contemplate unearthly, immaterial, and spiritual beauty as far as possible, as well as the reasons behind God's works. Being the first rank around God, they are instructed by the very source of mystery and are supremely guided in divine secrets. 

Theologians clearly show that the lower orders of heavenly beings are taught the divine sciences by their superiors in a specific order. Those who are higher than all others are illuminated by the Godhead itself, as far as is allowed, through revelations of divine mysteries. For instance, the scriptures describe some angels being religiously instructed by those of a higher rank: they learn that He who ascended to heaven in human form <gloss>1</gloss> is the Lord of the heavenly Powers and the King of Glory. 

Others are shown asking questions of Jesus Himself, desiring to learn exactly the nature of His divine work on our behalf. In these cases, Jesus instructs them directly, showing them the work of His goodness and His love for humanity at first hand. “For I,” He says, “am pondering righteousness and the judgment of Salvation.” <gloss>2</gloss> 

Now, I am astonished that even these first beings in Heaven—who are so far above all others—should reverently strive after divine illumination as though they were intermediate beings. For they do not ask directly, “Why is your clothing red?” <gloss>3</gloss> instead, they first raise the question among themselves...

<gloss>1</gloss> Psalm 24:7-10.

# CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.

The first Hierarchy, then, of the heavenly minds is purified, enlightened, and perfected by being religiously directed by the Author of initiation <note>In this context, the "Author of initiation" refers to God or Christ as the source of sacred mysteries.</note> Himself. This occurs through its immediate elevation to Himself and by being filled, according to its rank, with the most holy purification of the unapproachable Light of the most perfect source of initiation. It remains unstained by any inferiority and is full of primary Light, perfected by its participation in the first-given knowledge and science. To sum up, I may say—and not inappropriately—that the reception of Divine Science is at once purification, enlightenment, and perfecting. It purifies from ignorance by the knowledge of the more perfect revelations granted to it according to its fitness; it enlightens by that same Divine knowledge, through which it also purifies the order that did not previously contemplate the things now made manifest through higher illumination; and finally, it perfects through that same Light the enduring science of the mysteries now clearly revealed. 

This, then, according to my most accurate knowledge, is the first rank of the heavenly Beings which encircle and stand immediately around God. Without symbols and without interruption, this rank dances around His eternal knowledge in the most exalted and ever-moving stability. They view many blessed sights with pure eyes and are illuminated with unique and immediate splendours. They are filled with a Divine nourishment—abundant in its first-given profusion, yet one in the simple and unifying oneness of the Divine Food. Thus, they are deemed worthy of great participation and cooperation with God, by becoming like Him, as far as possible, in their excellent character and actions <note>original: "habits and energies."</note>. They know many Divine things pre-eminently and participate in Divine science and knowledge to the utmost degree. For this reason, the Word of God <note>meaning the Holy Scriptures</note> has transmitted their hymns to those of us on earth, in which the excellence of their most exalted illumination is divinely shown. For some of them—to speak in human terms—proclaim like the “voice of many waters,” 

> “Blessed is the glory of the Lord, from His place.”<gloss>1</gloss>

But others cry aloud that frequent and most majestic word of God, 

> “Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord of Hosts <note>original: "Sabaoth," a Hebrew term for "armies" or "hosts."</note>,” “The whole earth is full of His glory.”<gloss>2</gloss>

We have already explained these most excellent hymns of the super-celestial minds to the best of our ability in the *Treatise concerning the Divine Hymns*, and have spoken sufficiently concerning them in that work. From that treatise, for the sake of memory, it is enough to provide as much as is necessary for our present purpose—namely, “That the first Order, having

### CHAPTER VIII.

->*Concerning Lordships, Powers, and Authorities, and concerning their middle Hierarchy.*<-

Let us now pass to the middle order of the heavenly minds, gazing as far as we may with celestial vision upon those Lordships and the truly awe-inspiring <note>original: terrible</note> visions of the Divine Authorities and Powers. For each name of the minds above us indicates those characteristics by which they divinely imitate the Divine Likeness. 

I think, then, that the descriptive name of the Holy Lordships <note>term: Lordships; also known as "Dominions"</note> denotes a certain free <note>original: unslavish</note> tendency toward higher things, free from all groveling subservience. This rank does not submit in any way to any harsh force that is unlike itself, as fits the freedom of unbending Lordship. It is superior to every kind of cringing slavery, invincible to every lower tendency, and elevated above every difference <note>original: dissimilarity</note>. It ever seeks the true Lordship and the source of Lordship, molding itself and those below it into an image of goodness, according to His Lordly Likeness. It turns itself entirely away from things that only seem to exist, focusing instead on the Lordly Being and always sharing in the Lordly Likeness of God. 

The name of the Holy Powers <note>term: Powers; also known as "Virtues"</note> denotes the possession, to the highest possible extent, of a certain vigorous <note>original: masculine</note> and unflinching courage toward all those God-like energies within themselves. They are not weak or feeble in receiving any of the Divine illuminations granted to them, but vigorously conduct themselves in the imitation of God. They do not abandon their God-like movement through cowardice, but look unflinchingly toward the super-essential <note>term: super-essential; meaning existing beyond the category of "being" or "essence"</note> and powerful source of Power. They become an image of this, as far as is permitted, in the likeness of His power, and powerfully...

The name of the **Holy Authorities** denotes that they share the same rank as the Divine Lordships and Powers. It signifies their beautiful and orderly arrangement for receiving divine gifts and the organization of celestial and spiritual authority. They do not use their authoritative powers bossily <note>original: imperiously</note> for selfish or base purposes, but instead conduct themselves with the highest possible devotion to divine things in their proper order. They guide those beneath them with kindness and are made like the Source of all Authority, as much as is allowed. They make this authority visible to other angels through the well-ordered regulations that grant power. 

The middle rank <note>original: order</note> of the heavenly minds, having these God-like characteristics, is purified and enlightened in the manner described through the divine light granted to it at second hand. These illuminations pass through the first hierarchical order and then through this middle order as a secondary manifestation. 

Without dwelling too long on the idea of a message passing from one angel to another, let us take it as a symbol of a revelation delivered from a distance, which becomes slightly obscured as it becomes a secondary revelation. For, as those experts in our spiritual initiation say, the knowledge of divine things shown directly to us is more perfect than the divine truths shared through others. Therefore, I believe that regarding the angelic ranks, the direct participation in knowledge by those elevated closest to God is clearer than the knowledge of those who are initiated through the help of others. 

For this reason, in our priestly <note>original: sacerdotal</note> tradition, the first minds are called the perfecting, illuminating, and purifying powers of those below them. This is because the lower ranks are led through them to the Origin of all things, which is above all being <note>original: superessential Origin</note>. They are given access, as far as they are permitted, to the sacred purifications, enlightenments, and perfections. For it is a fixed law of divine consecration that the second rank should receive divine light through the first. 

You will find this declared by the theologians <note>referring here to the biblical writers</note> in many ways. For instance, when the Divine and Fatherly Love toward humanity had disciplined His people Israel to lead them toward spiritual salvation—delivering them to cruel and savage nations for correction through providential training—He eventually freed them from their misery. Through His compassion, He led them back to their former state of comfort. At that time, one of the theologians, Zechariah, saw in a vision one of the "first angels" who stands near God (for the name "Angel" is common to them all, as I said). This angel learned comforting words directly from God regarding this matter. But Zechariah also saw another angel, of...

Or concerning the angel of the first Rank <note>original: Order</note> who took the fire from the midst of the Cherubim? Or, even more remarkable than this, showing the good order that reigns among the Angels: the Cherubim cast the fire into the hands⁷ of the angel wearing the sacred stole. Or what of the one who called the most divine Gabriel and said to him, "Make this man understand the vision"?⁸ These, and whatever else is recorded by the holy theologians concerning the God-like order of the celestial hierarchies, show that the good order of our own priesthood <note>original: Hierarchy</note>, when made as similar to them as possible, will reflect the angelic beauty. It takes its entire form from this model and is led toward the transcendent <note>original: superessential</note> source of order found in every hierarchy.

## CHAPTER IX <note>original: CAPUT IX</note>

### *Concerning the Principalities, Archangels, and Angels, and regarding their Hierarchy, which is the last.*

There remains for our religious contemplation one final Rank which completes the Angelic Hierarchies; it is composed of the God-like Principalities, Archangels, and Angels. I think it is necessary...

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the third and final hierarchy of celestial beings: the Principalities, Archangels, and Angels. It explains their specific roles in mediating between the divine realm and the human world.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>

<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

[It is neces]sary, first, to explain the meaning of their sacred names <note>original: appellations</note>. For the name of the heavenly **Principalities** signifies their ability to rule and guide according to the Divine example. They maintain a sacred order most fitting for princely powers; they are turned entirely toward the "Prince above all Princes" <note>God</note> and lead others in a princely fashion. They are molded in the image of that sovereign Authority who is the source of all power, and they make His beyond-essential <note>original: superessential; referring to God's nature as being beyond the category of "being" or "essence"</note> source of order visible through the disciplined regularity of their own governing powers.

The Order of the Holy **Archangels** is of the same rank as the heavenly Principalities. As I have mentioned, they and the Angels together form a single hierarchy and rank. However, since there is no hierarchy that does not have a first, a middle, and a last power, the holy order of Archangels occupies the middle position in this hierarchy between the two extremes. It shares characteristics with both the most holy Principalities and the holy Angels. 

It relates to the Principalities because it is oriented in a princely way toward the sovereign Prince who is beyond all being, is modeled after Him as much as possible, and unites the Angels through its own well-regulated, organized, and invisible leadership. Yet, it also relates to the Angels because it belongs to the prophetic rank <note>original: Order</note>. It receives divine illumination from the first powers in a priestly <note>original: sacerdotal</note> fashion and conveys it to the Angels in imitation of God. Through the Angels, it reveals these truths to us in proportion to the sacred capacity of each godly person who is illuminated. 

As we have said before, the **Angels** complete the entire series of celestial minds, being the final order of heavenly beings who possess the angelic character. In fact, we more appropriately call them "Angels" <note>from the Greek 'angelos', meaning messenger</note> than we do those of higher degrees, specifically because their order is occupied with making things known and is more particularly concerned with the affairs of the world. 

We must consider that the highest rank <note>the Seraphim, Cherubim, and Thrones</note>, being placed first near the Hidden One, directs spiritual matters in a more concealed way than the rank immediately below it. The second rank—composed of the holy **Lordships, Powers, and Authorities**—directs the hierarchy of the Principalities, Archangels, and Angels more clearly than the first hierarchy does, yet more secretly than the order following it. 

We must keep in mind that this more "revealing" order of the Principalities, Archangels, and Angels presides over human hierarchies. They work through one another so that our instruction, conversion, communion, and union with God may happen in the proper order. In short, they ensure that the grace flowing from God—granted in a manner fitting His goodness to all hierarchies—reaches everyone in a sacred and regular sequence. For this reason, the Word of God <note>the Bible</note> has assigned our human hierarchy to the Angels, specifically naming the illustrious Michael as the Ruler of the Jewish people.

...others over other nations. For the Most High established the borders of nations according to the number of the Angels of God <note>original: Deuteronomy 32:8. This verse in the Septuagint version of the Bible suggests that God assigned an angel to watch over every nation.</note>. But if anyone should ask, “How then were the Hebrew people alone led toward Divine illuminations?” we must answer that we should not blame the faithful oversight of the Angels for the fact that other nations wandered after false gods. Rather, those nations themselves, by their own decline, fell away from the faithful path toward the Divine through self-conceit, self-will, and an irrational reverence for things they mistakenly thought were worthy of God.

Even the Hebrew people are recorded to have suffered this same failure; for He says, “You have cast away the knowledge of God, and have followed after your own heart” <note>original: Hosea 4:6 and Jeremiah 16:12</note>. For our lives are not governed by necessity, nor are the Divine rays of providential illumination dulled because of the free will of those who are under God's care. Instead, it is the unfitness of our own mental vision that makes the overflowing gift of Light—which comes from the goodness of the Father—either impossible to share in or impenetrable because of our resistance. It can also make the participation in that one fountain-like ray appear diverse: small or great, obscure or brilliant, even though that ray is single and simple <note>original: simplex; meaning unified and without parts</note>, always the same, and forever overflowing.

Even over the other nations—from whom we ourselves have emerged into that boundless and generous sea of Divine Light, which is open for everyone to receive—there were no "alien gods" placed in charge. Instead, there is one Prince of all, and the Angels who religiously direct each nation lead those who follow them toward Him. Let us consider Melchizedek <note>original: Genesis 14:18; Hebrews 7:1. A mysterious figure in the Bible who was a priest of God despite not being part of the Hebrew covenant.</note> as a Hierarch <term>a sacred leader or high priest</term> most dear to God; he was a priest not of non-existent gods, but of Him who is truly the Most High God. For those wise in godly matters not only speak of him as being dear to God, but also as a Priest, in order to show clearly to the wise that he was not only personally turned toward the true God, but also that he became a guide to others as a Hierarch, leading them in that wisdom which points to the true and only Godhead.

Let me also remind your hierarchical judgment of this—that the watchful and ruling care of Providence and the Lordship over all was made known through visions to both Pharaoh <note>original: Genesis 41:1–7</note>, by the Angel presiding over the Egyptians, and to the Babylonian Prince <note>original: Daniel 2:1</note>, by his own Angel. Furthermore, leaders who worshipped the true God were appointed over those nations. The interpretation of things revealed by these angelic visions was made known by God through Angels to holy men who were kindred to the Angels—men like Daniel and Joseph. For there is only one Prince and Providence over everyone. We must never think that the Godhead guides the Jews alone by lot <note>meaning by special chance or exclusive assignment</note>,...

It also shows that all the Angels who preside over each nation lead those who follow them with a willing mind as far as possible toward that Providence as their own proper Head.

## CHAPTER 10
<note>original: "CAPUT X"</note>

### *A Repetition and Summary concerning the Angelic Order.*

We have concluded, then, that the most exalted order of the minds <note>original: "minds"; referring to the celestial intellects or highest angels like the Seraphim</note> around God is purified, illuminated, and perfected by a gift of light. This happens while they are being sanctified by the perfecting illumination, which is at once more hidden and more manifest. It is more hidden because it is more intellectual, simplifying, and unifying; it is more manifest because it is the first gift and the first revelation, being more complete and poured out more fully upon them because they are more transparent. 

From this order, in due degree, the second order is led; from the second, the third; and from the third, our own human Hierarchy is religiously guided to the Source above all sources <note>original: "Beginning above beginning"</note> and the End of all good order. This follows the same law of well-ordered regularity in Divine harmony and proportion. 

Furthermore, all Angels are interpreters of those above them. The very highest are the interpreters of God, who moves them; the rest, in their proper order, are interpreters of those who have been moved by God. For the superessential <note>original: "superessential"; meaning existing beyond all essence or being</note> harmony of all things has provided for this religious order and the regulated conduct of...

<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

For each of the rational and intellectual beings, every rank of the Hierarchies has been arranged in a sacred order. We observe that every Hierarchy is distributed into first, middle, and last Powers. To speak more accurately, God divided each individual order itself by these same Divine harmonies. For this reason, theologians say that the most Divine Seraphim <note>original: "Seraphin"</note> cry out to one another,^1 which I believe indicates clearly that the first rank imparts its theological knowledge to the second. 

I might also appropriately add that every celestial and human mind possesses within itself its own specific first, middle, and last ranks and powers. These correspond to the specific instructions of the various hierarchical illuminations mentioned before, which are made known in their proper order. In accordance with this, each individual participates—as far as is lawful and permitted—in the purest cleansing, the most abundant light, and the highest perfection. For nothing is perfect in itself, or entirely without need of being perfected, except for the one Being who is inherently and supremely perfect.

## CHAPTER 11
<note>original: "CAPUT XI"</note>

### *Why all the celestial Beings are collectively called "Celestial Powers."*

Now that we have defined these matters, we should consider why we are accustomed to calling all the angelic beings collectively "celestial Powers." We cannot say here, as we do regarding the "Angels," that the specific Order of the holy Powers is the very last of all. <note>The author is comparing how the name of the lowest rank, "Angels," is often used for the whole group, and why "Powers"—a middle rank—is also used this way.</note> 

The orders of the superior beings share in the holy illuminations of the lower ranks, but the lower ranks do not share in the qualities of the superior ones. For this reason, all holy minds may be called "celestial Powers," but the lower ranks are never called "Seraphim," "Thrones," or "Lordships." This is because the lower ranks do not possess the full characteristics of the highest orders. 

Even though the Angels—and those above them, such as Archangels, Principalities, and Authorities—are placed after the rank of "Powers" by the Word of God <note>referring to the Bible</note>, we often refer to them all together as "heavenly Powers." However, we maintain that by using this common title, we do not intend to confuse the unique characteristics of each individual Order. 

In the celestial descriptions given of them, all divine minds are divided into three aspects: substance, power, and activity <note>original: "energy"</note>. Therefore, when we speak of all of them (or some of them) indiscriminately as "celestial Beings" or "celestial Powers," we should understand that we are describing them in a general way, based on their substance or their inherent power.

---

<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

each of them. We must not apply the superior characteristics of those holy Powers (whom we have already distinguished clearly enough) to the beings who are entirely inferior to them. To do so would disrupt the distinct order of the angelic ranks. According to the accurate explanation we have often given, the superior Orders possess the sacred qualities of the inferior ranks in abundance, but the lowest do not possess the complete excellence of the highest. The first revelations of light are revealed to the lower ranks through the first Order, in proportion to their capacity to receive them.

## CHAPTER 12
<note>original: "CAPUT XII"</note>

### *Why human Hierarchs are called Angels.*
<note>A "Hierarch" in this context refers to a high-ranking religious leader, such as a bishop, who oversees sacred rites.</note>

Diligent readers of the sacred scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note> sometimes ask this: Since the lowest Orders do not possess the full perfection of the superior ranks, why does scripture name our human Hierarch the "Angel of the Lord Almighty"? <note>original: "Angel of the Lord Omnipotent"</note><sup>1</sup> In my opinion, this statement does not contradict what we have previously defined. We say that the lower ranks lack the full and supreme power of the most exalted Orders, yet they possess these qualities in part and in proportion, according to the harmonious fellowship that binds all things together. 

For example, the Order of the holy Cherubim <note>original: "Cherubin"</note> possesses wisdom and knowledge to a higher degree. However, the Orders of beings beneath them also possess wisdom and knowledge, though only partially and to a lesser extent when compared to the Cherubim. The possession of wisdom is common to all spiritual beings that bear the image of God. But being "near and first," or "second and inferior," is not common; it is determined for each according to its own rank. One might safely apply this definition to all spiritual Minds: just as the higher ranks possess the good qualities of the lower ranks in abundance, the lower ranks possess those same good qualities—not in the same degree, but in a subordinate way. 

Therefore, I believe there is nothing absurd about the Word of God calling our Hierarch an "Angel." He participates, according to his own capacity, in the prophetic role of the Angels and elevates himself, as far as possible, to imitate their powers of interpretation. You will also find that the Word of God calls both the celestial beings above us and the holy men <note>original: "ἄνδρας"</note><sup>2</sup> among us "gods,"<sup>3</sup> because they are beloved by God. This is true even though the Divine Mystery <note>original: "Hiddenness"</note> is transcendently elevated above everything. No created being can properly or entirely be said to be like God, except for those intellectual and—

## CHAPTER 13
<note>original: "CAPUT XIII"</note>

### *Why the Prophet Isaiah is said to have been purified by the Seraphim.*

Let us now examine, as best we can, why one of the Seraphim <gloss>1</gloss> <note>Isaiah 6:6-7. The author uses "Seraphin" as the plural/class name.</note> is said to have been sent to one of the theologians <note>In this context, "theologian" refers to the prophet Isaiah as a speaker of divine things.</note>. Someone might object that it was not an inferior angel, but one of the highest-ranking beings who purified the prophet. 

Indeed, some argue that, based on the established relationship between all celestial minds, this scripture <note>original: "portion of the Word of God"</note> does not mean one of the highest beings surrounding God came to purify the theologian. Instead, they suggest that one of the angels assigned to us as a sacred minister for the prophet's purification is called by the same name as the Seraphim. This is because the removal of sin and the restoration of the purified person to divine obedience <gloss>2</gloss> <note>Isaiah 6:7</note> was accomplished through fire. They say this prophecy <note>original: "oracle"</note> refers simply to "one of the Seraphim"—not one of those who stand directly before God, but one of the Powers placed over us for the purpose of purification. 

However, another person offered me a very reasonable defense of this position. He argued that the great angel who created this vision—whoever he may have been—aimed to teach the theologian divine truths. To do this, he attributed his own purifying function first to God, and after God, to the primary, highest-ranking Hierarchy. And is this statement not true? For the one who said this affirmed that the divine power reaches everyone through its frequent visitations and penetrates all things irresistibly, yet it remains invisible to all. This is not only because it is elevated in its essence above everything <note>original: "superessentially elevated"</note>, but because it secretly transmits its providential energies to all. Rather, it is revealed to all spiritual beings in the proper measure, providing its gift of light to the most venerable beings. Through these beings of the first order, the light is distributed in proper sequence to the lower orders, according to each rank's capacity to behold the vision of God. 

To speak more precisely and use familiar examples (for even if they fall short of the glory of God, who is exalted above all, they are more helpful for us to understand): the transmission of a sunbeam passes most easily through matter of the first order—meaning substance that is

<meta>This page continues the explanation of how divine illumination flows from God through the highest angels (the first hierarchy) down to the lower ranks and eventually to humans, using the analogy of light and heat passing through different materials.</meta>

<page-num>40</page-num>

<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

...is more transparent than all others, and through this it shines its own splendor more brilliantly. But when it strikes denser matter, its transmitted brilliance becomes more obscured because that matter is unsuitable for passing on the gift of light. Because of this, the light is gradually blocked until it is almost perfectly prevented from passing through. 

Similarly, the heat of fire transmits itself primarily to things that are more receptive, yielding, and able to become like the fire itself. But when it touches repellent substances, it leaves either no trace or only a very slight trace of its fiery energy. Furthermore, when fire comes into contact with things that do not naturally catch fire, it acts through substances that *are* favorable to its action. For example, if it ignites materials that change easily under heat, and through them heats water or another substance that does not heat easily, it warms them in proportion to their capacity to receive it. 

Following this same rule of Nature's orderly system, the primary law of all good order—both visible and invisible—supernaturally reveals the brightness of its own gift of Light to the most exalted beings in abundant streams of direct revelation <note>original: "first-manifestation"</note>. Through these beings, the beings below them participate in the divine ray. Because these higher beings are the first to know God and strive pre-eminently for divine virtue to become "primary workers" <term>original: "first-workers"; those who perform divine acts or "theurgy"</term>, they are deemed worthy of the power and energy to imitate God to the greatest possible degree. 

These higher beings lovingly lift up the beings below them to an equality with themselves as far as possible, ungrudgingly sharing the splendor that rests upon them; these beings, in turn, share it with those subordinate to them. Throughout every rank, the first level shares its gift with the one that follows. Thus, with providential foresight, the divine light rests upon everyone in the proper proportion. 

Therefore, for all those who are illuminated, there is one Source of illumination: God. This is true naturally, really, and properly, as God is the Essence of Light and the Cause of existence and vision itself. But, by divine decree and through the imitation of God, the superior rank acts as the source for the rank that follows it, because the divine rays are poured through the higher to the lower. All remaining angels, then, naturally look to the highest rank of heavenly minds as the source of all sacred knowledge and the imitation of God, since divine illumination is distributed to everyone—even to us—through them. For this reason, they attribute every holy act of divine imitation to God as the *Cause*, but to the first Godlike minds as the primary authors and teachers of divine things.

The first rank of holy angels possesses the characteristic of fire more than any other. They also possess the distribution of the streams of divine wisdom, the faculty of knowing the highest science of divine illuminations, and the characteristic of **Thrones** <note>The "Thrones" are one of the ranks in the highest triad of angels, often associated with being a seat or foundation for God's presence.</note>, showing how they expand to receive God. But the ranks of the subordinate beings also possess the faculty of fire, of...

The person who spoke these things used to claim that this vision was shown to the Prophet <note>original: "Theologian," referring here to the biblical author Isaiah</note> through one of the holy and blessed angels who are appointed over us. It was through this angel’s illuminating guidance that the Prophet was raised to that spiritual vision in which he saw the most exalted beings—to speak symbolically—positioned under God, with God, and around God <note>original: "John i. 1." The text draws a parallel to "The Word was with God."</note>. He saw the Supreme Eminence, who is beyond all greatness, indescribably elevated above everything and seated on high in the middle of the most exalted beings. 

The Prophet then learned from what he saw that the Divine is seated above every visible and invisible power, possessing a pre-eminence that is beyond all essence <term>original: "superessential"</term>. He saw that God is exalted above all as Absolute—not even comparable to the highest created beings. Furthermore, he learned that God is the very essence of all things, the Cause of every cause, and the unchanging center of the unending existence of all. From Him, the most exalted powers receive both their existence and their well-being. 

Then he was taught about the God-like qualities of the most holy Seraphim, whose sacred name signifies "The Fiery Ones." We shall soon discuss them to show, as best we can, the hidden mysteries of that fiery <note>original: "empyrion"</note> faculty which leads toward the imitation of God. 

The holy Prophet observed the free and most exalted return to the Divine, represented by the first, middle, and last ranks of the six wings. He contemplated their countless feet, many faces, and extended wings—some under their feet, others over their faces, while they perpetually moved their middle wings. Through this, he was brought to a spiritual understanding of what he saw. The vision revealed to him the power of these most exalted minds for deep insight and vast contemplation. It showed the sacred reverence they hold, far above earthly beings, and their bold, courageous—yet impossible—scrutiny into higher and deeper mysteries. It also showed their tireless and high-flying movement in their orderly pursuit of imitating God. 

He was also taught the hidden mysteries of that divine and highly esteemed heavenly hymn of praise <note>The "Sanctus": "Holy, Holy, Holy," from Isaiah 6:3</note>. The angel who presented the vision shared his own divine knowledge with the Prophet as much as possible. He also taught him that participating in the divine and radiant purity, as much as one is able...

...is a purification for the pure, at whatever level they may be. But because this purification comes from the Godhead itself through the most exalted causes to all the sacred Minds by a supernatural hiddenness, it is, in a sense, more manifest. It reveals and distributes itself in a higher degree to the highest powers that surround it. 

However, regarding the second rank, or us—the lowest intellectual powers—as each of us is distant from the Divine likeness, God contracts His brilliant manifestation into the single unknowable nature of His own hiddenness. He illuminates the second rank specifically through the first rank; and, to speak briefly, He is first brought from hiddenness into manifestation through those first powers. 

The Prophet <note>original: "Theologian," referring to Isaiah</note> was taught this by the Angel who was leading him to the Light—that purification and all Divine activities <term>original: "energies"</term>, once they are made known through the first Beings, are distributed to all the rest according to how ready each is to share in the process of becoming like God <term>original: "deifying participations"</term>. Therefore, the characteristic of purification by fire was naturally attributed to the Seraphim, who rank just after God. It is not absurd, then, to say that a Seraphim purifies the Prophet. 

For just as God purifies everyone by being the cause of all purification—or rather (to use a familiar illustration), just as our Leader <term>original: "Hierarch"; in this context, a bishop or high priest</term>, when purifying or enlightening through his deacons or priests, is said to purify and enlighten himself, because the orders consecrated through him attribute their own specific functions to him—so too the Angel who carried out the purification of the Prophet attributed his own purifying knowledge and power to God as the Cause, but to the Seraphim as the first-operating Leader. One might say, with Angelic reverence, while teaching someone being purified:

> “There is a pre-eminent Source, Essence, Worker, and Cause of the cleansing performed on you through me. It is He who brings the first Beings into existence, holds them together by their fixed position around Himself, and keeps them without change or fall, moving them to be the first to share in His own providential activities <term>original: "energies"</term>. For the one who taught me these things used to say that this shows the mission of the Seraphim. Now, that Leader and Guide who ranks after God—the Glory of the most exalted Beings, from whom I was taught to purify according to the example of God—this is He who cleanses you through me. Through Him, the Cause and Creator of all cleansing brought forth His own caring activities from the hidden Abyss, even to us.”

He taught me these things, and I pass them on to you. Let it be a part of your spiritual and discerning skill either to see that each of the reasons given is free from objection (honoring one over the other as having more likelihood, good reason, or perhaps the truth), or to find out something for yourself that is closer to the real truth, or to learn it from someone else. God, of course, gives the Word, and Angels supply it. Then, you may reveal to us—the friends of Angels—a more luminous view, if it should be so, which would be especially welcome to me.

## CHAPTER 14 <note>original: "CAPUT XIV"</note>

->*What the number attributed to the Angels signifies.*<-

In my opinion, it is also worth considering spiritually that the teachings of the scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note> regarding the Angels state that they are "thousands upon thousands" and "myriads upon myriads." By adding and multiplying our highest numerical limits, they clearly show that the ranks of the heavenly Beings cannot be counted by us. The blessed hosts of the celestial minds are many, far surpassing the weak and limited measurement of our earthly calculations. However, they are precisely known by the heavenly intelligence and knowledge given to them in abundance by the supreme Divine and infinitely wise Source <note>original: "Head"</note>—the essential Cause, connecting Power, and final Limit of all created things combined. (Daniel 7:10.)

Come then, if you please, let us finally rest our minds from the strain of those lofty contemplations that are suited for Angels. <meta>This page continues the discussion of Chapter 15, which explores the symbolic physical descriptions of angels. It specifically addresses how different ranks of angels relate to one another in hierarchy and begins an in-depth analysis of why fire is the primary symbol used for celestial beings in the Bible.</meta>

<page-num>44</page-num>
<header>CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.</header>

The explanations of these symbolic likenesses <note>original: "typical likenesses"</note> represent the same ranks of the celestial Beings as sometimes ruling, and at other times as being ruled; and the last as ruling, and the first as being ruled. They are also described, as has been said, as having first, middle, and last powers. This description does not introduce anything absurd into the account if we follow this explanation: if we were to say that some are ruled by those above them, and then that they ruled those same superiors—or that the superiors, while ruling those below, were ruled by those very subordinates—the idea would clearly be absurd and full of confusion.

But if we say that the same beings rule and are ruled, but not in the same way or by the same entities—instead, each Order is itself ruled by those above it, but rules those below it—one might say appropriately that the Divinely inspired forms in the scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note> may sometimes properly and truly apply to both first, middle, and last powers. Now, the act of rising by turning toward things above, and being drawn firmly around each other as guardians of their own specific powers, belongs truly to all the heavenly Beings. So too does the possession of the power to provide for those after them through mutual communication. This is true for all, though it applies to some primarily <note>original: "pre-eminently"</note> and completely, as we have often said, but to others partially and to a lesser degree.

But we must keep our discussion within bounds. In our first explanation of these symbols <note>original: "types"</note>, we must search for the reason why the Word of God prefers the sacred representation of fire above almost every other.<sup>1</sup> You will find it representing not only wheels of fire, but also living creatures of fire;<sup>2</sup> and men flashing like lightning;<sup>3</sup> and placing heaps of burning coals around the heavenly Beings themselves;<sup>4</sup> and rivers of flame flowing with irresistible force.<sup>5</sup> It also says that the thrones are made of fire;<sup>6</sup> and it shows from their name that the most exalted Cherubim <note>The author likely refers to the Seraphim here, whose name means "burning ones," though the text says Cherubin.</note> glow with fire. It attributes the characteristics and activity <note>original: "energy"</note> of fire to them;<sup>7</sup> and everywhere, above and below, it prefers primarily the representation through the image of fire.

I think, then, that the symbol <note>original: "similitude"</note> of fire<sup>8</sup> indicates how closely the Divine minds resemble God. Holy theologians frequently describe the Being who is beyond all essence <note>original: "superessential"</note> and formless through the image of *fire*, as it has many similarities to the Divine character in things visible. For physical fire is, so to speak, in everything and passes through everything without becoming part of it <note>original: "unmingled"</note>. It is raised above all things. While giving light to everything, it is at the same time concealed, remaining unknown in its essential nature unless it happens to show its own specific power in some material thing nearby.

near it. It is both incomprehensible and invisible; it subdues all things and brings under its own power <note>original: "energy"</note> anything it happens to touch. It is unchangeable, sharing itself with all things near it, whatever they may be. It renews things by its rousing heat and gives light through its illuminations, which cannot be hidden. It is uncontrollable, pure <note>original: "unmingled"</note>, separating, unchangeable, uplifting, penetrating, and high—subject to no lowly inferiority. When unobserved, it seems not to exist, but it manifests itself suddenly—according to its own nature—through friction, as if we were searching to find it. Then, flying away uncontrollably, it remains undiminished throughout all its joyful sharing of itself. One might find many characteristics of fire that are appropriate for displaying Divine Power through images perceptible to the senses <note>original: "sensible images"</note>. Those, then, who are wise in the things of God, knowing this, depict the celestial beings using the image of fire, showing their God-like nature and their imitation of God as far as is allowed.

But they also depict them in the likeness of men <gloss>1</gloss> because of their intellectual nature, their ability to look toward things above, and their straight, upright form. They also represent the innate faculty of ruling and guiding; for while humans are physically weaker than irrational animals <note>original: "irrational Beings"</note>, they rule over all through the superior power of the mind, the excellence of rational science <note>In this context, "science" refers to the systematic application of reason and knowledge.</note>, and the natural freedom <note>original: "unslavishness"</note> and indomitable spirit of the soul. It is possible, I think, to find within each of the many parts of our body images that correspond to the heavenly powers. For instance, we can say that the power of vision <gloss>2</gloss> signifies the most clear elevation toward the Divine light, as well as a sensitive, pure, and open reception—free from all worldly passion—of the supreme Divine illuminations.

But the discriminating powers of the nostrils signify the ability to receive the distributed fragrances, sweet beyond imagination, and the power to distinguish accurately those things that are not sweet, entirely rejecting them <gloss>3</gloss>.

The powers of the ear signify the conscious participation in and reception of Divine inspiration.

The powers of taste signify the fullness of intellectual food and the reception of Divine, nourishing streams <gloss>4</gloss>.

The powers of touch signify the skillful ability to distinguish between what is helpful or harmful <gloss>5</gloss>.

<page-num>46</page-num>

# CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.

The eyelids and eyebrows signify the guarding of the mental concepts which reveal God.

The figures of manhood and youth represent the perpetual bloom and vigor of life. <note>Refers to Mark 16:5, where a young man in a white robe is seen at the tomb.</note>

The teeth signify the dividing of the perfect nourishment given to us. For each intellectual Being <note>original: "intellectual Being"; referring here to an angel</note> divides and multiplies—by a caring faculty—the unified conception given to it by the more Divine Order. This is done in the proportion necessary for the instruction of those below them.

The shoulders and elbows, and further, the hands, represent the power of making, striving, and accomplishing.

The heart is a symbol of the God-like life, dispersing its own life-giving power to the objects of its concern, following the Divine likeness.

The chest, in turn, signifies the invincible and protective faculty of the life-giving distribution, as it is placed above the heart.

The back represents the holding together of all the productive powers of life.

The feet signify movement, quickness, and the skill of the perpetual motion that advances toward Divine things. This is why the Word of God represented the feet of the holy Minds <note>original: "holy Minds"; a term for angels</note> as being placed under their wings. For the wing displays the quickness of instruction, the progress toward heavenly and higher things, and the elevation above all earthly things out of love for what is higher. The lifting up of the wings signifies that they are in no way earthly, but are raised in all purity to higher things without hindrance.

To be naked and unshod <note>original: "unshod"; i.e., barefoot</note> signifies being unfettered, agile, unrestrained, and free from all external excess; it represents their likeness to Divine simplicity as far as it can be attained.

But since the "varied wisdom of God" <note>original: "simple and variegated wisdom"; Refers to Ephesians 3:10</note> both clothes the naked and gives them certain tools to carry, let us explain, as best we can, the sacred garments and tools of the celestial Minds.

The shining and glowing clothing, I believe, signifies the Divine likeness following the image of fire—which is brilliant—and their sharing of Light because of their rest in Heaven, where Light dwells. It represents their complete ability to illuminate as superior beings, and their capacity to be illuminated as inferior ones. 

The priestly robe <note>original: "sacerdotal robe"</note> signifies their instruction in Divine and mystical visions, and the dedication <note>original: "consecration"</note> of their entire life. The girdles signify the guard they keep over their productive powers, and their own focused habit of being turned toward Oneness with God.

...encircle it in good order, being drawn around itself by an unchanging identity.

The rods signify the kingly and directing ability that sets all things straight. The spears <note>original: "spears"; see Genesis 3:24</note> and the battle-axe <note>original: "battle-axe"; see Ezekiel 9:2</note> symbolize the separation of things that are different, and the sharp, energetic, and powerful action of the power of discernment. The geometrical <note>original: "geometrical"; see Ezekiel 11:3</note> and technical tools <note>original: "technical"; see Amos 7:7</note> represent founding, building, and completing, along with everything else that belongs to the teaching and guiding care provided to the lower ranks of angels. However, sometimes the tools of the holy angels shown to us are symbols of God's judgment upon us. Some represent His corrective <note>original: "correcting"; see Numbers 22:31</note> instruction or His avenging justice <note>original: "righteousness"; see 2 Kings 24:16</note>. Others symbolize freedom from danger, the completion of one's training, the restoration of former well-being, or the addition of other gifts—whether small or great, physical or spiritual <note>original: "intellectual"; see Zechariah 3:9</note>. A discerning mind would not have any difficulty in properly connecting these visible symbols to invisible realities.

The fact that they are called winds <note>original: "winds"; see Psalm 104:3 and Daniel 7:2</note> signifies their rapid flight, moving almost instantly to all things. It also describes their movement as they pass from above to below, and then from below to above—lifting the lower ranks to the heights above, while moving the higher ranks to a shared and caring guidance of the lower orders. Perhaps one might also say that calling an aerial spirit a "wind" signifies the divine likeness of these heavenly minds. This also serves as a likeness and model of divine energy (as I have explained more fully in my work *Symbolic Theology* regarding the four elements), matching the moving, life-giving, rapid, and unstoppable progression of nature, as well as the unobserved and invisible mystery of its origins and endings. For the Scripture says, "You do not know where it comes from or where it goes" <note>original: "Thou knowest not whence it cometh nor whither it goeth"; see John 3:8</note>. Furthermore, the Word of God describes them as having the appearance of a cloud <note>original: "cloud"; see Ezekiel 10:4</note>. This signifies that these holy minds are filled to a supernatural degree with hidden light. They receive this first revelation without pride and distribute it generously to the ranks below them as a secondary revelation, according to their ability to receive it. Moreover, it shows that a productive, life-giving, nurturing, and perfecting power is stored within them. This is like the spiritual production of rain, which calls the receptive womb of the earth, through fruitful showers, to the life-giving labor of birth.

The Word of God also attributes to the heavenly beings a similar—

<meta>This page continues the exploration of symbolic imagery used to describe celestial beings, moving from tools and natural elements to the significance of precious metals, stones, and animal forms found in the visions of the prophets.</meta>

<page-num>48</page-num>

# CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.

...likeness to brass <note>original: "Brass"; likely referring to burnished bronze as seen in Ezekiel 1:7</note>, electron <note>original: "Electron"; an alloy of gold and silver known as electrum, or perhaps amber</note>, and many-colored stones. Electrum, being a mixture of silver and gold, represents the incorruptible nature of gold—with its inexhaustible, undiminished, and spotless brilliance—and the luminous, heavenly radiance of silver. But to brass, for the reasons already given, we must attribute either the likeness of fire or that of gold.

We must consider that the many-colored appearances of stones denote various qualities: white represents the luminous; red, the fiery; yellow, the golden; and green represents the youthful and the full-grown. Within each of these likenesses, you will find an explanation that teaches the inner meaning of these symbolic images.

But since I believe this has been sufficiently explained according to our ability, we will pass to the sacred explanation of the divine representations of the celestial minds through wild beasts. We must consider that the shape of a **Lion** signifies leadership, strength, and an indomitable nature. It also represents the effort to become as much like the unutterable Deity as possible by "concealing one’s spiritual footsteps"—referring to the mysteriously secret way the path to God is covered during divine illumination.

The image of the **Ox** denotes strength and maturity, as if turning up spiritual furrows in the soul to receive heavenly and productive powers. Its horns represent a guarding and unconquerable nature.

The representation of the **Eagle** signifies kingship and soaring high; it represents swiftness, alertness, agility, and cleverness in searching for the nourishment that provides strength. It also symbolizes an unimpeded, straight, and unflinching gaze toward the generous and brilliant splendor of the divine rays of the sun, maintained through the robust endurance of its vision.

The imagery of **Horses** represents obedience and docility. When they are **white**, it signifies brilliance and especially their affinity to the Divine Light. When they are **black**, it represents the "Hidden" or the mysterious. When they are **red**, it represents the fiery. When they are **black and white** <note>original: "black and white"; likely referring to dappled or piebald horses</note>, it represents the power that connects extremes, joining the first to the second and the second to the first by directing or intertwining them. 

If we did not have to limit the length of this discourse, we might appropriately apply the particular characteristics of these living creatures and all their bodily representations to the heavenly powers based on the principle of **"dissimilar similitudes"** <note>A key concept for the author: using "unlike" or "low" physical images to describe "high" spiritual truths, which prevents us from thinking God actually looks like these things</note>. For instance, their appearance of **anger** represents spiritual courage (of which physical anger is but a distant echo); their **desire** represents Divine love. To speak summarily, we refer all the physical senses and body parts of irrational beings to the immaterial concepts and unified powers of the celestial beings. But not only is this sufficient for the...

<meta>This page concludes the analysis of symbolic imagery—specifically rivers, chariots, and wheels—before addressing the nature of angelic joy and concluding the discussion on divine representations.</meta>

<page-num>49</page-num>

# CELESTIAL HIERARCHY.

...Likewise, even an explanation of one of the dissimilar representations would be enough for an accurate description of similar things in the same fashion. However, we must examine the fact that **Rivers** <note>Daniel 7:10</note> are mentioned, as well as **Wheels** <note>Ezekiel 10:9</note> and **Chariots** <note>2 Kings 2:11</note>, as being associated with the heavenly beings. 

The **rivers of fire** signify the divine streams that provide the angels with a generous and incessant flow, nourishing the productive powers of life. The **chariots** represent the shared communion of those of the same rank in united fellowship. The **wheels**, being described as winged and advancing without turning or deviating, denote the power of their energy to move forward in a straight and direct path, while their entire intellectual track is divinely directed toward that same unwavering goal. 

It is also possible to interpret the sacred description of the spiritual wheels in another way, for the name **Gel, Gel** <note>original: *Gel, Gel*; from the Hebrew *galgal*, meaning a wheel or whirlwind, as found in Ezekiel 10:13</note> is given to them, as the sacred writer says. In the Hebrew language, this term suggests both "revolutions" and "revelations." The divine wheels of fire have "revolutions" through their perpetual movement around the Goodness <note>referring to God</note> itself; they have "revelations" by explaining hidden mysteries, instructing those on earth, and by the downward flow of exalted illuminations to the beings below. 

There remains one final point for an accurate explanation: the statement regarding the **rejoicing of the celestial orders** <note>Luke 15:10</note>. These beings are utterly incapable of our kind of emotional or physical pleasure. Rather, they are said to "rejoice with God" over the discovery of what was lost. This reflects their divine, good-natured, and God-like generous joy over the care and salvation of those who turn toward God—a joy beyond description that holy men and women also often experience when the divinizing lights of the Deity rest upon them. 

Let it be enough, then, to have said this much concerning these divine representations. While this no doubt falls short of a perfectly accurate explanation, I believe it will prevent us from "servilely resting" <note>meaning to get stuck in a literal or superficial understanding</note> in the outward appearance of these symbols. If you should argue that we have not mentioned every angelic power, energy, or likeness described in the scriptures <note>original: "oracles"</note>, we answer in truth that we do not possess the celestial knowledge of some; for those, we would need another instructor to guide us into the Light. Other things we have omitted because they are similar to what has already been said, or to keep this discourse from becoming too long. As for the hidden mysteries that are beyond our capacity, we have honored them with silence.
