<margin>1 Matthew 16:17</margin>
<margin>2 Luke 10:16</margin>

...are retained." And to the one who was illuminated with the Divine revelations of the most Holy Father, the Scriptures <note>original: "Oracles"</note> say, "Whatever you bind on earth shall be bound in heaven; and whatever you loose on earth shall be loosed in heaven." This is because he, and every Hierarch <note>A high-ranking leader in the church, such as a bishop</note> who is like him, receives those who are dear to God according to the revelations of the Father's judgments resting upon him. He acts by announcing and interpreting the Divine Will, and he rejects those who are without God. Furthermore, as the Scriptures affirm, he uttered that sacred and divine saying not of his own accord <note>original: "not as self-moved"</note>, nor as though "flesh and blood" had revealed it to him, but as one who had been spiritually taught divine things by God. The inspired Hierarchs, then, must exercise their decisions and all their hierarchical powers just as the Godhead, the Supreme Initiator, moves them. As for everyone else, they must follow the Hierarchs in their priestly <note>original: "sacerdotal"</note> functions as people moved by God. "For he who despises you," He says, "despises Me."

Let us now move on to what follows the prayer we mentioned. When the Hierarch has finished this, he first greets the deceased <note>original: "defunct"</note>, and then everyone else present. For anyone who has been perfected in a Godly life is dear and honored to all people who are like God. After the greeting, the Hierarch pours oil upon the deceased. But remember that during the process of "Birth from God"—before the most Divine Baptism—the oil of Chrism <note>Consecrated oil used in religious rites</note> is given to the neophyte <term>a new convert or newly baptized person</term> as a first step in participating in the holy symbol, after their old clothes have been completely removed. But now, at the conclusion of everything, the oil is poured upon the deceased. The first anointing with oil calls the person being initiated to holy struggles; but the pouring of oil now shows that the deceased has successfully finished those same struggles and has been made perfect.

When the Hierarch has finished these tasks, he places the body in an honorable tomb <note>original: "chamber"</note> alongside the bodies of other holy men of the same rank. For if a person has lived a life dear to God in both soul and body, then the body that struggled alongside the devout soul will also be honored. Therefore, Divine justice gives the soul its mutual inheritance along with its own body, as they were companions and partners in either a devout life or its opposite. For this reason, the Divine institution of sacred rites bestows the most divine communions upon them both—to the soul through pure contemplation and precise knowledge of the ceremonies being performed; and to the body by sanctifying the entire person through the symbolic use of the most Divine Myrrh <term>a fragrant oil</term> and the most holy symbols of Divine Communion. This effects his complete salvation and announces that his resurrec—

---

1 Matthew 16:17. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; 2 Luke 10:16.

<summary>This page explains that a Hierarch's authority to forgive or retain sins comes from divine revelation rather than personal will. It also details the funeral rite of anointing the deceased with oil, symbolizing the completion of a life of spiritual struggle and the sanctification of both body and soul.</summary>

<keywords>Hierarch, binding and loosing, funeral rites, anointing, oil of Chrism, body and soul, Divine Will, Myrrh, baptismal symbolism</keywords>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the final rites and transitions into explaining the secrecy of certain prayers and the justification for infant baptism within the Church's structure.</meta>

# ECCLESIASTICAL HIERARCHY.

...sanctification will be most complete by entire sanctification. Now, as regards the consecrating invocations <note>Special prayers used to bless or set someone apart for a holy purpose</note>, it is not permitted to explain them in writing, nor may we bring their mysterious meaning—nor the divine powers working within them—out of their secrecy and into the public. But, as our sacred tradition holds, by learning these through quiet instruction, and by being perfected to a more God-like condition and elevation through Divine love and holy duties, you will be carried by the most perfect enlightenment to the most exalted knowledge of them.

Now the fact that even children, who are not yet able to understand the Divine mysteries, should become recipients of the holy Birth from God <note>This refers to the sacrament of Baptism</note> and of the most holy symbols of the supremely Divine Communion <note>The Eucharist or Lord's Supper</note> seems, as you say, a fit subject for ridicule to the uninitiated <note>original: "profane"</note>. They operate on the assumption that the Hierarchs <note>High-ranking church leaders, such as bishops</note> teach Divine things to those who are unable to hear, and vainly pass on the sacred traditions to those who do not understand. And this is considered even more laughable—that others, on the children's behalf, repeat the renunciations <note>original: "abjurations"; the formal rejection of the devil and sin</note> and the sacred covenants. 

But your Hierarchical judgment must not be too hard upon those who are led astray; instead, you should persuasively—and for the purpose of leading them to the light—reply affectionately to the arguments they allege. You should bring forward this fact, in accordance with sacred rule: that not all Divine things are within the grasp of our knowledge. Many things unknown to us have causes worthy of God which, although unknown to us, are yet well known to the Orders <note>The different ranks of angels and heavenly beings</note> above us. Many things also escape even the most exalted Beings, and are known distinctly only by the All-Wise and Wisdom-Giving Deity. 

Furthermore, concerning this practice, we affirm what our God-like instructors conveyed to us, which they had learned from ancient <note>original: "archaias"</note> tradition. For they say—and it is a fact—that infants being brought up according to a Divine institution will attain a religious character free from all error, and will be inexperienced in an unholy life. When our Divine leaders came to this conclusion, it was determined to admit infants upon the following conditions: that the parents of the child to be presented should entrust him to someone among the initiated who is a faithful teacher of children in Divine things. The child would then lead the rest of his life under this person's instruction, as if under a godfather and a guarantor for his religious safety. The Hierarch requires this person, who promised to bring up the child according to the religious life, to pronounce the renunciations and the religious confessions of faith. This is not, as critics would jokingly say, a matter of instructing one person instead of another in Divine things. For the godfather does not say, "On behalf of this child, I personally make this renunciation and this sacred profession."

---

$^1$ **Ancient** <note>original Greek: *archaias*</note>. See Acts 15:7, 21, 16; and Archbishop Trench. Yet even Dupin <note>Louis Ellies Dupin (1657–1719), a French ecclesiastical historian</note> ignorantly claimed that word as proof that the text was written after the time of the Apostles. *New Bibliography*, p. 106.

<summary>This page defends the practice of infant baptism against skeptics, explaining that children are placed under the care of a godfather to ensure a holy upbringing. It also emphasizes that certain "consecrating invocations" are too sacred to be written down and must be learned through spiritual growth.</summary>
<keywords>infant baptism, Hierarch, godfather, renunciations, sacred tradition, mysteries, consecration, Louis Ellies Dupin</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page concludes the author's defense of infant baptism and the role of godparents, ending the treatise on the Ecclesiastical Hierarchy, followed by the translator's closing signature and a list of his other published works.</meta>

# ECCLESIASTICAL HIERARCHY.

<sig>N</sig>

...but rather that the child, through the sponsor, renounces evil and gives consent. That is to say: "I promise to persuade the child, once he has reached a state of mind capable of religious understanding, through my instructions in divine truths, to entirely renounce contrary influences and to profess and perform the divine confessions." There is nothing irrational, then, in my judgment, if the child is raised with a religious education, having a guide and religious sponsor <note>original: "surety"; a person who takes responsibility for another's religious upbringing, similar to a godparent</note> who instills in him a love for divine things and keeps him protected from worldly influences.

The Hierarch <note>A high-ranking church leader, such as a bishop</note> grants the child the sacred symbols <note>The sacraments, specifically the Eucharist or Holy Communion</note> so that he may be spiritually nourished by them and lead no other life but one of constant contemplation of divine things. By participating in these rites as he grows in faith, the child may develop a religious character and be devoutly raised by his god-guided sponsor. These, my son, are the beautiful and consistent visions of our Church structure <note>original: "Hierarchy"</note> which have been presented to my view. Perhaps others with more clear-sighted minds have viewed these things not only more clearly, but also more divinely. I imagine that even more brilliant and divine beauties will shine forth for you as you use these thoughts as stepping stones toward a higher light. Share your own more perfect enlightenment with me as well, and show me the more beautiful and consistent truths you have been able to see; for I am confident that, by what has been said, I shall ignite the sparks <note>original: "strike the sparks"</note> of the Divine Fire that lie dormant within you.

->THANKS BE TO GOD.<-

*October 27th, 1893.* ->JOHN PARKER.<-

---

¹ Francis Bacon, *The Advancement of Learning*, p. 2.

NOTE 2.—*Thanksgiving before the consecration, translated from a Latin Version of the Liturgy of Dionysius the Areopagite.*

> The priest, bowing low <note>original: "Sacerdos inclinatus"</note>: For truly the celebration of Your benefits, O Lord, exceeds the powers of mind, expression, and thought; nor can any lip, mind, or tongue worthily praise You. For by Your word the heavens were made, and by the breath of Your mouth all the heavenly <note>original: "supernal"</note> powers, all lights which are in the sky—sun and moon, sea and dry land, and whatever is in them. Things without voice, by their silence—and articulate things by their voice through speech and hymns—perpetually praise You, because You are essentially good and above all praise, existing incomprehensibly in Your essential nature. (Elevating his voice.) This tangible creation <note>original: "sensible creation"; meaning creation that can be perceived by the senses</note> praises You, O Lord, as well as those spiritual Beings <note>original: "intellectual Beings"</note> placed above our material senses. Heaven and earth glorify You. Sea and air proclaim You. The sun in its predestined course praises You. The moon in its regulated changes venerates You. The multitudes of Archangels and the hosts of Angels, and those Virtues <note>One of the nine choirs, or ranks, of angels</note> seated above the world and the power of our understanding, send their blessing to Your throne. The Splendors of exceeding and hidden light send forth their "Holy" <note>original: "Sanctus"</note> to Your glory. Principalities and Orders praise You with joyful shouts <note>original: "Jubilate"</note>; Powers and Lordships venerate You. Virtues, Thrones, and Seats inaccessible—bright images of eternal light, mirrors without flaw, holy beings and recipients of sublime wisdom, preeminently searchers after that will kept secret from all—these exalt You by clear movements of matchless tones, and by voices worthy of rational creatures. Many-eyed Cherubim, most highly exalted, bless You. Seraphim, with their enfolded wings and many feathers, call You Holy—those same beings who cover their faces with their wings and veil their feet with their wings, and flying around and clapping their wings one to another, so that they may not be consumed by Your devouring heat, sing sweet and...

<summary>This page contains a list of the early Bishops of Paris and Arles followed by a liturgical prayer of thanksgiving from the Liturgy of Dionysius the Areopagite, which describes the celestial hierarchy of angels praising God.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the appendix with the final portion of the Liturgy of Dionysius, focusing on the Sanctus (the "Holy, Holy, Holy"), the nature of the Trinity, the Incarnation, and the Institution of the Eucharist. It ends with a note on the text's historical origins.</meta>

# APPENDIX.

> ...pure chants in a universal harmony, offering eternal glory to You, crying out in a hymn worthy of God, and saying (People): “Holy, Holy, Holy.”
>
> (Priest bending low.) You are Holy, God the Father, Omnipotent Maker and Creator of every creature—invisible, visible, and perceivable by the senses <note>original: "sensible"</note>. You are Holy, Only-begotten Son—Power and Wisdom of the Father, Lord and our Savior Jesus Christ. You are Holy, God the Holy Spirit, Perfecter and Sanctifier of Saints. Holy and Indivisible Trinity, co-essential <note>original: "co-essential"; meaning of the same substance or essence</note> and of equal glory, Whose compassion toward our race is abundantly poured forth; You are Holy and the one who sanctifies all. You have not left our human race in exile from Paradise when it had become entangled in every kind of sin, but have been manifested to it by the Word, which endured extreme poverty before the world began. That Nature the Word took—made in all things like unto it, sin excepted—so that He might make it prepared for holiness and disposed to this life-giving feast. (Raising his voice.) He who was conceived, formed, and molded by the Holy Spirit, both from the Virgin blood of the Virgin Mary, Holy Mother of God, and from the pure and most holy body of the same, was indeed born as Man, as the Author of life. Receiving the Godhead in the flesh (while the laws and properties of Nature were preserved, yet in a manner above Nature), He was acknowledged as God in Spirit and Man in flesh. And inasmuch as the Word existed before the ages <note>original: "In quantum erat Verbum ante Sæcula"</note> from You, in a manner worthy of God, He was born; and by signs and miracles such as became the Maker of All, He was proved to be such, since He has abundantly provided complete healing and perfect salvation for the whole human race. But at the end and completion of His own dispensation <note>original: "dispensation"; here meaning the divine plan or management of human salvation</note>, for our sake and prior to His own saving Cross, He took Bread into His own pure holy hands and looked up to You, God the Father. Giving thanks, He blessed, sanctified, and broke it, and gave it to His own disciples, the holy Apostles, saying, “Take and eat from this, and believe <note>original: "Credite quod corpus meum est illud ipsum quod pro vobis"; "Believe that this is my body, the very same which [is given] for you"</note> that this is My Body, which for you and for many is broken and given for the expiation of faults, the remission of sins, and eternal life.”

**N.B.**—I give this as an example from a Liturgy full of interest and preserved in the East, known as the Liturgy of Dionysius, Bishop of Athens and a disciple of St. Paul. <note>This refers to the "Liturgy of St. Dionysius," an ancient Eastern Christian service. Modern scholars generally attribute such works to "Pseudo-Dionysius" rather than the biblical disciple.</note>

->**THE END.**<-
